Use when asked about GPU selectors, attributes, capacity, sharing, passthrough, scheduling, allocation failures, or container-visible devices.\n---\n\n# DRA GPU Allocation Workflows\n\n## Purpose\n\nTrace a GPU request from declared intent through scheduler allocation and node preparation to the exact device, sharing, and CDI result.\n\n## Triggers\n\n- Explain or create a GPU, MIG, VFIO, time-slicing, or MPS claim.\n- Diagnose Pending pods, unallocated claims, prepare/unprepare failures, missing devices, or stale inventory.\n- Review DeviceClasses, ResourceSlice attributes/capacity, CEL selectors, opaque configuration, or feature-gate constraints.\n- Analyze dynamic MIG lifecycle, checkpoint recovery, or CDI injection.\n\n## Audience\n\n- Contributors\n- Cluster operators\n- Support engineers\n\n## Inputs\n\n- Driver/chart and Kubernetes versions\n- GPU model, MIG/VFIO state, NVIDIA driver, runtime/CDI, and feature gates\n- ResourceClaim or ResourceClaimTemplate, Pod, DeviceClass, and ResourceSlice output\n- Pod/claim events, gpu-kubelet-plugin logs, checkpoint symptoms, and container evidence\n- Whether GPU Operator manages the surrounding stack\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Verify current behavior from `site/content/docs/concepts/gpu-allocation.md`, the relevant guide/reference pages, and code. Do not extrapolate planned release behavior.\n2. Classify the requested resource:\n   - Full GPU: `gpu.nvidia.com`\n   - MIG slice: `mig.nvidia.com`\n   - VFIO passthrough: `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com`\n3. Identify the sharing/config contract from `api/nvidia.com/resource/v1beta1/`:\n   - `GpuConfig`\n   - `MigDeviceConfig`\n   - `VfioDeviceConfig`\n   - Sharing and IOMMU settings\n4. Validate required feature gates and every dependency or mutual exclusion against `pkg/featuregates/featuregates.go` and current docs. Treat code/docs differences as drift to report.\n5. Inspect the matching DeviceClass templates and the node's `gpu.nvidia.com` ResourceSlice. Distinguish serialized bare attribute keys from CEL access through `device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com']` and capacity through `device.capacity['gpu.nvidia.com']`.\n6. Trace the allocation path:\n   - Pod references a ResourceClaim or template.\n   - The scheduler matches DeviceClass, CEL selectors, attributes, and capacity against ResourceSlices and records the allocation/node.\n   - Kubelet calls the GPU plugin's prepare path.\n   - The plugin validates config and device state, performs any MIG/VFIO/sharing setup, persists prepared state, writes CDI specs, and returns CDI device names.\n   - The runtime consumes CDI and starts the container.\n   - Unprepare reverses driver-owned state when the claim is released.\n7. For static MIG, confirm partitions existed before plugin discovery and restart the plugin after approved out-of-band hardware changes. For dynamic MIG, verify advertised partitionable capacity, Kubernetes gate/version requirements, on-demand creation, teardown, and checkpoint recovery.\n8. For VFIO, verify IOMMU, driver binding, feature gates, workload/runtime expectations, and processes that may hold the GPU. Treat host driver changes as privileged operations.\n9. For sharing, distinguish time-slicing, MPS, MIG hardware isolation, and plain multi-container use of the same claim. Do not describe them as equivalent isolation models.\n10. Diagnose in layers: DeviceClass → ResourceSlice inventory/health → claim allocation → scheduler events → node assignment → plugin prepare → checkpoint/CDI → runtime/container.\n11. Use maintained samples under `demo/specs/` and docs under `site/content/docs/guides/gpu-allocation/`; adapt API versions to the target Kubernetes release.\n12. Return a request-to-injection explanation or bounded triage report with prerequisites, observed evidence, unsupported assumptions, and validation steps.\n\n## Expected Outputs\n\n- GPU claim or selector design\n- Request-to-CDI flow explanation\n- Allocation triage report\n- Compatibility and validation checklist\n\n## References\n\n- GPU concepts: device count, selectors, opaque config, feature gates, and expected sharing/isolation semantics.\n- Confirm the allocated device and node from claim status and ResourceSlice data.\n- Confirm prepare succeeded and a CDI device reached the intended container.\n- For the ADR scenario, explain ResourceClaim → scheduler binding → kubelet prepare → CDI injection and name the evidence available at each boundary.\n- For dynamic MIG, verify actual partition creation and later teardown, not only scheduler allocation.\n- For failures, separate driver behavior from Kubernetes scheduler, GPU Operator, This page explains what they are, how they differ, and how Kubernetes schedules them.\n\n| DeviceClass | Resource type | Use case |\n|---|---|---|\n| `gpu.nvidia.com` | Full GPU | Exclusive or shared access to a single physical GPU |\n| `mig.nvidia.com` | MIG slice | Hardware-isolated partition of a supported GPU |\n| `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com` | VFIO passthrough | Raw GPU access for workloads that manage the driver themselves |\n\n---\n\n## Resource types\n\n### Full GPUs\n\nA full GPU gives a container exclusive access to a single physical GPU. This is the default allocation mode and requires no additional configuration.\n\nFull GPUs support optional sharing for cases where you want to divide the GPU across multiple workloads:\n\n- **Consumable capacity:** independent `ResourceClaim` objects can share a multi-allocatable device while Kubernetes tracks the capacity consumed by each claim.\n  The NVIDIA driver requires its `ConsumableShares` feature gate and a `consumableShares` mode.\n- **Time-slicing:** containers take turns on the GPU using CUDA preemption. Requires the `TimeSlicingSettings` feature gate.\n- **MPS (Multi-Process Service):** containers run concurrently with configurable thread percentage and memory limits. Requires the `MPSSupport` feature gate.\n\nTarget DeviceClass: `gpu.nvidia.com`\n\nSee [`demo/specs/quickstart/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/{{< param driver_release_tag >}}/demo/specs/quickstart) for configuration examples.\n\n---\n\n### MIG slices\n\nMIG (Multi-Instance GPU) partitions a MIG-capable NVIDIA data center GPU (such as A100, A30, H100, and newer) into independent hardware slices. Each slice has its own dedicated compute engines, memory bandwidth, and L2 cache — workloads are fully isolated at the hardware level.\n\nUnlike time-slicing and MPS, MIG partitioning is enforced by the GPU hardware itself. A container claiming a MIG slice cannot be affected by activity on another slice of the same physical GPU.\n\nMIG slices are identified by profiles such as `1g.5gb` or `3g.20gb`, where the first number is the fraction of GPU compute and the second is the dedicated memory allocation. The available profiles depend on the physical GPU model.\n\n#### Static and dynamic MIG\n\nMIG support has two modes:\n\n- **Static MIG:** the node administrator pre-configures MIG partitions using\n  `mig-parted` or `nvidia-smi`. The driver discovers existing partitions and\n  exposes them as allocatable devices. This is enabled by default; no feature\n  gate is required.\n- **Dynamic MIG:** the driver creates and destroys partitions automatically\n  based on workload requests. Requires the `DynamicMIG` feature gate.\n\nStatic and dynamic MIG differ only in *who* creates the partitions, not in how\nyou request them. The same `ResourceClaimTemplate` and pod manifests work in\nboth modes.\n\n#### How static MIG discovery works\n\nStatic MIG requires no feature gate and no MIG-specific driver configuration.\nThe driver always discovers pre-configured MIG partitions, so a default install\n(`resources.gpus.enabled=true`) is all that is needed on the driver side. All of\nthe setup happens on the node, before the driver starts.\n\nThe GPU kubelet plugin discovers partitions at startup and advertises each one as\na device in the node's `ResourceSlice`:\n\n- Discovery is a point-in-time snapshot. Partitions added or changed after the\n  driver starts are not picked up until the GPU kubelet plugin restarts.\n- MIG mode enabled with no partitions strands the GPU. If a GPU has MIG mode\n  enabled but no partitions configured, the driver advertises neither the full\n  GPU nor any slices for it, and logs a warning.\n\n#### How dynamic MIG works\n\nWith dynamic MIG, the driver creates and destroys partitions automatically based\non workload requests, so you do not pre-configure them on the node.\n\nDynamic MIG builds on the Kubernetes partitionable devices feature (KEP-4815).\nIt works in three phases:\n\n1. **Advertise every possible partition.** At startup, the GPU kubelet plugin\n   enumerates all MIG profiles and every valid placement for each profile on\n   each GPU, then advertises all of them in the node's `ResourceSlice`, even\n   though none exist on the hardware yet. Alongside the devices, it publishes\n   shared counters (one set per physical GPU) that track compute, memory, and\n   memory-slice usage. Each advertised partition declares how much of those\n   counters it would consume. This lets the scheduler allocate a partition that\n   does not yet exist, and prevents handing out two partitions that would\n   physically overlap on the same GPU.\n2. **Create the partition on demand.** After the scheduler allocates a partition\n   to a pod, the GPU kubelet plugin creates the actual MIG device when it\n   prepares the pod's resources on the node — enabling MIG mode on the GPU if\n   needed, then creating the GPU instance and compute instance for the requested\n   profile and placement.\n3. **Destroy the partition when it is no longer needed.** When the claim is\n   released, the driver tears the MIG device back down. The plugin uses a\n   node-local checkpoint as the source of truth, so it can reclaim partitions\n   left behind by a crash or partial preparation when it restarts.\n\nA request can be satisfied even when no matching partition was pre-configured —\nthe driver creates one to match.\n\nMIG slices support consumable capacity, time-slicing, and MPS sharing, using the same approaches as full GPUs.\nTime-slicing on MIG slices does not support interval configuration.\n\nTarget DeviceClass: `mig.nvidia.com`\n\nSee the [Request full GPUs guide](../guides/gpu-allocation/allocating-gpus.md) and\n[`demo/specs/quickstart/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/{{< param driver_release_tag >}}/demo/specs/quickstart)\nfor GPU configuration examples. For MIG setup and workload examples, see the [MIG guide](../guides/gpu-allocation/mig.md).\n\n---\n\n### VFIO passthrough\n\nVFIO (Virtual Function I/O) passes a full physical GPU directly to a container, bypassing the NVIDIA driver stack in the host kernel. This gives the container raw hardware access and is intended for workloads that manage the GPU driver themselves, such as virtual machine managers or specialized research environments.\n\nVFIO passthrough has no sharing options; one container gets one GPU.\n\nTarget DeviceClass: `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com`\n\nRequires the `PassthroughSupport` feature gate (Alpha, default: false). See [Feature gates](../reference/feature-gates.md) to enable it.\n\n---\n\n## Choosing a resource type\n\n| | Full GPU | MIG slice | VFIO passthrough |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Hardware isolation | No | Yes | Full device |\n| Sharing supported | Yes (consumable capacity, time-slicing, or MPS) | Yes (consumable capacity, time-slicing, or MPS) | No |\n| Supported hardware | All NVIDIA GPUs | MIG-capable data center GPUs (A100, A30, H100, and newer) | All NVIDIA GPUs |\n| Feature gate required | No (sharing gates optional) | No | `PassthroughSupport` (Alpha) |\n| Typical use case | ML training, general workloads | Multi-tenant inference, strict isolation | VM passthrough, specialized environments |\n\n### Add optional allocation features\n\nChoose a resource type first, and then add only the features that apply to that\nresource:\n\n- **Sharing across claims:** Kubernetes DRA consumable capacity lets independent claims share a full GPU or MIG device by memory capacity, without a capacity limit, or by a fixed share count.\n  Refer to [Consumable capacity](../guides/gpu-allocation/consumable-capacity.md).\n- **Sharing within a claim:** time-slicing or MPS can share supported full-GPU or MIG allocations among containers that reference the claim.\n  A device configuration uses one sharing strategy.\n- **Fabric topology:** Fabric Manager partitioning constrains a claim to one\n  complete NVSwitch partition.\n  The partitioning applies to full GPUs and VFIO devices, not MIG slices.\n  `PassthroughSupport` is required if you want to use VFIO devices.\n- **Operational features:** health checking and device metadata modify\n  supported workflows without creating another resource type.\n\nRefer to [Feature gate constraints](../reference/feature-gates.md#constraints)\nfor the combinations enforced at driver startup.\nFor the topology workflow, refer to\n[Fabric Manager partitioning](../guides/gpu-allocation/fabric-manager-partitioning.md).\n\n---\n\n## How scheduling works\n\nWhen a pod references a `ResourceClaim`, Kubernetes uses the following flow to allocate and inject the GPU:\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant User\n    participant API as Kubernetes API\n    participant Scheduler\n    participant Kubelet\n    participant Plugin as GPU Kubelet Plugin\n    participant Runtime as Container Runtime\n\n    User->>API: Create Pod + ResourceClaim\n    API->>Scheduler: Schedule pod\n    Scheduler->>Plugin: Query available ResourceSlices\n    Scheduler->>API: Bind ResourceClaim to device + node\n    API->>Kubelet: Start pod on bound node\n    Kubelet->>Plugin: NodePrepareResources (claim + device + config)\n    Plugin->>Plugin: Generate CDI spec\n    Plugin->>Kubelet: Return CDI device names\n    Kubelet->>Runtime: Start container with CDI devices\n    Runtime->>User: Container running with GPU\n```\n\nThe key components in this flow:\n\n- **ResourceSlice:** the kubelet plugin publishes each node's GPU devices in a single pool (one or more ResourceSlices), advertising the devices it makes allocatable and their attributes.\n- **ResourceClaim:** the user's request for a specific type of GPU device, optionally with a configuration (GpuConfig, MigDeviceConfig, or VfioDeviceConfig) embedded as opaque parameters.\n- **CDI (Container Device Interface):** the standard used to inject devices into containers. The kubelet plugin generates a CDI spec at prepare time; the container runtime reads it to set up the device files, environment variables, and mounts.\n\n---\n\n## Publishing GPUs in ResourceSlices\n\nThe GPU kubelet plugin discovers the GPUs on each node and publishes them as `ResourceSlice` objects. One pool per node, all under the `gpu.nvidia.com` driver. The make up of a `ResourceSlice` depends on how each GPU is configured:\n\n- A GPU available as a full device is published with device `type: gpu` and you can request it through the `gpu.nvidia.com` DeviceClass.\n- A GPU with MIG mode enabled is published as its MIG slices instead of a full GPU. Each MIG slice is assigned `type: mig` and you can request it through the `mig.nvidia.com` DeviceClass. With dynamic MIG, the driver also advertises every possible MIG partition plus per-GPU shared counters. See [How dynamic MIG works](#how-dynamic-mig-works) above.\n- A GPU prepared for VFIO passthrough is published with device `type: vfio` and you can request it through the `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com` DeviceClass.\n\nThe driver discovers the node's GPUs once, when the GPU kubelet plugin starts, and that inventory stays fixed while the plugin runs. It republishes `ResourceSlices` at runtime to reflect allocation, as devices are claimed and released, and, if `NVMLDeviceHealthCheck` is enabled, device health changes. The GPU kubelet plugin does not re-scan hardware. If you make a manual change to a node's GPU or MIG configuration, for example, reconfiguring MIG with `nvidia-smi`, you must restart the GPU kubelet plugin for the changes to take effect in the resource pool.\n\nThe `ResourceSlices` contain the attributes and capacity details for all the GPUs on your cluster. To review the `ResourceSlices` on your cluster, see [View available GPU resources](../guides/gpu-allocation/view-resources.md); for what each attribute and capacity field means, see [ResourceSlice device attributes](../reference/resourceslice-attributes.md).\n\n\n## Requesting a GPU\n\nResource request contains the following information:\n\n*  A `ResourceClaimTemplate` (or `ResourceClaim`) references one of the available DeviceClasses\n* Optionally, CEL selectors match the GPU attributes your workload requires\n* Optionally, an opaque driver configuration block for `GpuConfig`, `MigDeviceConfig`, or `VfioDeviceConfig` sharing and device behavior.\n\nThe scheduler uses these details to pick a matching device from the node's ResourceSlice.\n\nRefer to the [Kubernetes DRA documentation](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/) for details on the  claim and templating mechanics.\n\nFor more details on using these with the DRA, refer to the how-to guides:\n\n- [Request full GPUs](../guides/gpu-allocation/allocating-gpus.md)\n- [View available GPU resources](../guides/gpu-allocation/view-resources.md)\n- [MIG](../guides/gpu-allocation/mig.md)\n- [Time-slicing](../guides/gpu-allocation/time-slicing.md)\n- [Fabric Manager partitioning](../guides/gpu-allocation/fabric-manager-partitioning.md)\n- [VFIO GPU passthrough](../guides/gpu-allocation/kubevirt-vfio-gpu-passthrough.md)\n--- /Users/bmozaffari/Binaries/NVIDIA/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/site/content/docs/reference/api.md\n---\ntitle: API reference\nlinkTitle: API reference\nweight: 10\ndescription: Types and fields for the resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1 API group.\n---\n\nAll types belong to API group `resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1`.\n\n## GPU opaque configuration types\n\nThese types are set as opaque configuration in `ResourceClaim` and `ResourceClaimTemplate` specs to configure GPU resources. They are optional; omit them to use driver defaults. Some fields require feature gates to be enabled.\n\n### GpuConfig\n\nConfigures a full GPU device. \nTarget DeviceClass: `gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n  timeSlicingConfig:\n    interval: Default       # Default | Short | Medium | Long\n```\n\nWith MPS (Multi-Process Service):\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50             # optional, integer\n    defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit: \"4Gi\"         # optional, quantity\n    multiUser: true                               # optional, default false\n    defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit:            # optional, map\n      \"0\": \"2Gi\"\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Sharing strategy and its configuration. Omit to use the device exclusively. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS` (Multi-Process Service). |\n| `sharing.timeSlicingConfig.interval` | string | Time-slice duration: `Default`, `Short`, `Medium`, or `Long`. Requires the [`TimeSlicingSettings`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultActiveThreadPercentage` | integer | Thread percentage limit applied to all processes sharing the GPU. Requires the [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit` | quantity | Pinned memory limit applied to all devices. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit` | map | Per-device override of `defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit`. Keys are device index (integer) or UUID string. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.multiUser` | bool | Starts the MPS control daemon in multi-user mode so processes with different user IDs can share the device, defaults to `false` when omitted, and requires a Volta or newer GPU. |\n\n\n### MigDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a MIG device slice. Target DeviceClass: `mig.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n```\n\nWith MPS:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50\n    multiUser: true\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Supports `TimeSlicing` and `MPS` strategies. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS`. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig` | object | Supports the same MPS fields, defaults, and Volta-or-newer `multiUser` requirement as `GpuConfig.sharing.mpsConfig` and requires [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md). |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\n`timeSlicingConfig` is not available for MIG devices. Time-slicing on MIG slices does not support interval configuration.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### VfioDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a VFIO passthrough device. Target DeviceClass: `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: VfioDeviceConfig\niommu:\n  backendPolicy: LegacyOnly   # LegacyOnly | PreferIommuFD\n  enableAPIDevice: false       # optional\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `iommu` | object | Optional. IOMMU backend configuration. Omit to use defaults (`LegacyOnly`, API device disabled). |\n| `iommu.backendPolicy` | string | `LegacyOnly` (default) or `PreferIommuFD`. Selects the IOMMU backend used for device passthrough. |\n| `iommu.enableAPIDevice` | bool | Optional. Expose `/dev/iommu` or `/dev/vfio/vfio` to the workload. Defaults to `false`. Requires the [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`PassthroughSupport`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false). `iommu.enableAPIDevice` additionally requires [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n## ComputeDomain CRDs\n\nComputeDomains use two Custom Resource Definitions. Unlike the GPU opaque types above, these are concrete Kubernetes resources that exist independently of `ResourceClaim` specs.\n\n### ComputeDomain\n\nA user-created resource that provisions an ephemeral multi-node NVLink fabric. Creating one triggers the controller to deploy a per-domain IMEX daemon fleet and generate `ResourceClaimTemplate` objects that workload pods reference to claim IMEX channels. The spec is immutable after creation.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: ComputeDomain\nmetadata:\n  name: my-compute-domain\nspec:\n  numNodes: 0\n  channel:\n    resourceClaimTemplate:\n      name: my-channel\n    allocationMode: Single        # Single (default) | All\n```\n\n#### Spec fields\n\n| Field | Type | Default | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `spec.numNodes` | integer | — | Deprecated. Set to `0` when using the default [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. Formerly used to gate workload startup until a specific number of IMEX daemons joined. |\n| `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate.name` | string | — | Name of the `ResourceClaimTemplate` the controller creates for workload pods to claim a channel from. |\n| `spec.channel.allocationMode` | string | `Single` | `Single` allocates one IMEX channel per claim. `All` allocates the maximum number of channels available in the IMEX domain. |\n\n#### Status fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `status.status` | string | `Ready` when all expected IMEX daemons have joined; `NotReady` otherwise. |\n| `status.nodes[].name` | string | Node name. |\n| `status.nodes[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `status.nodes[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier for the node. |\n| `status.nodes[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Used to map IPs to DNS names. |\n| `status.nodes[].status` | string | Per-node daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nDo not gate workload startup on `status.status`. The status field is informational. IMEX daemons start as soon as their local node joins without waiting for all peers.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### ComputeDomainClique\n\nA `ComputeDomainClique` is a driver-managed resource created automatically by the IMEX daemon on each node. You do not create or modify these directly.\n\nEach `ComputeDomainClique` is namespaced to the driver namespace and named as `<computeDomainUID>.<cliqueID>`. It tracks which nodes have joined a specific NVLink clique within a `ComputeDomain` and their daemon readiness.\n\nThe ComputeDomain plugin reads these objects to verify that the local IMEX daemon is ready before allowing a workload container to start.\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`ComputeDomainCliques`](feature-gates.md) (Beta, default: true), which depends on [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `daemons[].nodeName` | string | Node name. |\n| `daemons[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `daemons[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier. |\n| `daemons[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Maps to a DNS name. |\n| `daemons[].status` | string | Daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. Defaults to `NotReady`. |\n\n\n## ComputeDomain opaque configuration types\n\nThe following opaque types are set in the `ResourceClaim` specs generated by the ComputeDomain controller. They are managed automatically, you do not set these directly. They are documented here for reference and debugging.\n\n### ComputeDomainChannelConfig\n\nOpaque configuration for IMEX channel `ResourceClaim` objects. Set by the controller in the `ResourceClaimTemplate` it generates from `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate`.\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `domainID` | string | UID of the parent `ComputeDomain` resource. |\n| `allocationMode` | string | Inherited from `spec.channel.allocationMode`. `Single` or `All`. May be absent when `Single` (the default). |\n\n### ComputeDomainDaemonConfig\n\nOpaque configuration for IMEX daemon `ResourceClaim` objects. Set by the controller in the daemon DaemonSet.\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `domainID` | string | UID of the parent `ComputeDomain` resource. |\n--- /Users/bmozaffari/Binaries/NVIDIA/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/site/content/docs/reference/resourceslice-attributes.md\n---\ntitle: ResourceSlice device attributes\nlinkTitle: ResourceSlice attributes\nweight: 40\ndescription: NVIDIA GPU, MIG, and VFIO device attributes and capacity published by the DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs in ResourceSlices.\n---\n\nThe DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs publishes each allocatable device — full GPUs, MIG\nslices, and VFIO passthrough devices — as an entry under `spec.devices` in a\nnode's `ResourceSlice`. This page is a reference for the NVIDIA-specific\nattributes and capacity on those entries: what each field means and the exact\nnames you reference in CEL selectors.\n\nFor the generic `ResourceSlice` type schema — every field on `ResourceSlice`,\n`Device`, `DeviceAttribute`, `DeviceCapacity`, and related types — see the\n[Kubernetes API reference for ResourceSlice v1](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubernetes-api/resource/resource-slice-v1/).\nFor how the driver publishes slices, see\n[Publishing GPUs in ResourceSlices](../concepts/gpu-allocation.md#publishing-gpus-in-resourceslices).\nTo inspect the ResourceSlices on your own cluster, see\n[View available GPU resources](../guides/gpu-allocation/view-resources.md).\n\n## Device types\n\nThe DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs publishes all devices under a single driver,\n`gpu.nvidia.com`, with one pool per node. The NVIDIA-specific `type` attribute on\neach device identifies the kind of device, and the built-in DeviceClasses select\non it:\n\n| `type` | DeviceClass | Device |\n|---|---|---|\n| `gpu` | `gpu.nvidia.com` | Full physical GPU |\n| `mig` | `mig.nvidia.com` | MIG slice |\n| `vfio` | `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com` | VFIO passthrough device |\n\nThe sections below show a representative `spec.devices[]` entry for each type.\n`kubectl` prints map keys alphabetically (so `type` and `uuid` appear last), the\n`#` comments are annotations rather than part of the real output, and the values\nare illustrative — confirm them on your own cluster.\n\n## Full GPU (type: gpu)\n\n<!-- gpuModuleID with ID, cmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/deviceinfo.go:236 -->\n\n```yaml\n- attributes:\n    addressingMode:\n      string: HMM                   # memory addressing mode, when available\n    architecture:\n      string: Ampere                # GPU architecture\n    brand:\n      string: Nvidia                # GPU brand\n    cudaComputeCapability:\n      version: 8.0.0                # CUDA compute capability\n    cudaDriverVersion:\n      version: 13.0.0              # CUDA driver version\n    driverVersion:\n      version: 580.126.20          # NVIDIA driver version\n    gpuModuleID:\n      int: 1                        # Fabric Manager GPU module ID, when enabled\n    partition2:\n      int: 4                        # ID of a reported size-2 FM partition\n    productName:\n      string: NVIDIA A100-PCIE-40GB # product name reported by NVML\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pciBusID:\n      string: 0000:65:00.0          # PCI bus address in BDF notation, when available\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pcieRoot:\n      string: pci0000:64            # PCIe root complex identifier, when available\n    resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode:\n      int: 0                         # NUMA node, when available\n    type:\n      string: gpu                   # device kind: gpu, mig, or vfio\n    uuid:\n      string: GPU-2fa81118-5a5f-aa66-7660-471eed407181\n  capacity:\n    memory:\n      value: 40Gi                   # total GPU memory\n    # On MIG-capable GPUs with partition metadata, additional capacities\n    # (multiprocessors, copyEngines, decoders, encoders, jpegEngines, ofaEngines)\n    # may also appear here.\n  name: gpu-0\n```\n\n## MIG slice (type: mig)\n\n```yaml\n- attributes:\n    addressingMode:\n      string: HMM\n    architecture:\n      string: Ampere                # inherited from the parent GPU\n    brand:\n      string: Nvidia\n    cudaComputeCapability:\n      version: 8.0.0\n    cudaDriverVersion:\n      version: 13.0.0\n    driverVersion:\n      version: 580.126.20\n    parentUUID:\n      string: GPU-2fa81118-5a5f-aa66-7660-471eed407181  # physical GPU hosting this instance\n    productName:\n      string: NVIDIA A100-PCIE-40GB # inherited from the parent GPU\n    profile:\n      string: 1g.5gb                # MIG profile, e.g. 1g.5gb or 3g.20gb\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pciBusID:\n      string: 0000:65:00.0\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pcieRoot:\n      string: pci0000:64\n    resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode:\n      int: 0                         # inherited from the parent GPU, when available\n    type:\n      string: mig                   # device kind\n    uuid:\n      string: MIG-1a2b3c4d-5e6f-7a8b-9c0d-1e2f3a4b5c6d\n  capacity:\n    copyEngines:\n      value: \"1\"                    # dedicated copy engines\n    decoders:\n      value: \"0\"                    # dedicated video decoders\n    encoders:\n      value: \"0\"                    # dedicated video encoders\n    jpegEngines:\n      value: \"0\"                    # dedicated JPEG engines\n    memory:\n      value: 4864Mi                 # dedicated slice memory (usable amount, below the \"5gb\" label)\n    multiprocessors:\n      value: \"14\"                   # streaming multiprocessors dedicated to the slice\n    ofaEngines:\n      value: \"0\"                    # dedicated optical-flow accelerators\n  name: gpu-0-mig-1g.5gb-0\n```\n\n## VFIO passthrough (type: vfio)\n\n```yaml\n- attributes:\n    deviceID:\n      string: \"0x20b0\"              # PCI device ID\n    gpuModuleID:\n      int: 1                         # Fabric Manager GPU module ID, when enabled\n    iommuFDEnabled:\n      bool: true                    # whether the IOMMUFD backend is enabled\n    partition1:\n      int: 8                         # ID of the size-1 Fabric Manager partition\n    partition2:\n      int: 4                         # ID of the size-2 Fabric Manager partition\n    partition4:\n      int: 2                         # ID of the size-4 Fabric Manager partition\n    partition8:\n      int: 1                         # ID of the size-8 Fabric Manager partition\n    productName:\n      string: NVIDIA A100-PCIE-40GB # product name reported by NVML\n    resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode:\n      int: 0                         # NUMA node, when available\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pciBusID:\n      string: 0000:65:00.0          # PCI bus address in BDF notation, when available\n    resource.kubernetes.io/pcieRoot:\n      string: pci0000:64            # PCIe root complex identifier, when available\n    type:\n      string: vfio                  # device kind\n    uuid:\n      string: GPU-2fa81118-5a5f-aa66-7660-471eed407181\n    vendorID:\n      string: \"0x10de\"             # PCI vendor ID (0x10de = NVIDIA)\n  capacity:\n    addressableMemory:\n      value: 40Gi                   # addressable device memory\n  name: gpu-vfio-0\n```\n\n## NUMA locality\n\nThe GPU kubelet plugin publishes the standard\n`resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode` attribute for full GPUs, MIG devices, and\nVFIO devices when the PCI NUMA node is available and non-negative.\n\nBy default, the attribute uses the scalar `int` form shown in the examples.\nWhen you enable both the driver and Kubernetes `DRAListTypeAttributes` feature\ngates, the ResourceSlice serialization changes to a one-element `ints` list:\n\n```yaml\nresource.kubernetes.io/numaNode:\n  ints:\n  - 0\n```\n\nThis representation change matters when inspecting or parsing ResourceSlices,\nbut it does not change the attribute name that you specify in ResourceClaims,\nlike the following example:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1\nkind: ResourceClaimTemplate\nmetadata:\n  name: same-numa-gpus\nspec:\n  spec:\n    devices:\n      requests:\n      - name: gpus\n        exactly:\n          deviceClassName: gpu.nvidia.com\n          allocationMode: ExactCount\n          count: 2\n      constraints:\n      - requests:\n        - gpus\n        matchAttribute: resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode\n```\n\nThis constraint requires all devices selected for `gpus` to have the same\npublished NUMA value. It uses the same\n`resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode` spelling for both the scalar `int` and\none-element `ints` representations.\n\n### Troubleshooting: verify the NUMA value\n\nList each GPU device with its node, PCI bus ID, and published NUMA value:\n\n```bash\nkubectl get resourceslices -o json | jq -r '\n  (\n    [\"NODE\", \"DEVICE\", \"PCI_BUS_ID\", \"PUBLISHED_NUMA\"],\n    (\n      .items[]\n      | select(.spec.driver == \"gpu.nvidia.com\")\n      | .spec.nodeName as $node\n      | .spec.devices[]\n      | .basic.attributes as $attrs\n      | select($attrs[\"resource.kubernetes.io/pciBusID\"] != null)\n      | ($attrs[\"resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode\"] // {}) as $numa\n      | [\n          $node,\n          .name,\n          $attrs[\"resource.kubernetes.io/pciBusID\"].string,\n          (\n            if $numa.int != null then ($numa.int | tostring)\n            elif $numa.ints != null then ($numa.ints | join(\",\"))\n            else \"omitted\"\n            end\n          )\n        ]\n    )\n  )\n  | @tsv'\n```\n\nFor one row, use its `NODE` and `PCI_BUS_ID` values to read the host PCI\ndevice's NUMA node:\n\n```bash\nNODE=<node-from-output>\nBDF=<pci-bus-id-from-output>\n\nkubectl debug \"node/${NODE}\" -it --image=ubuntu -- chroot /host \\\n  cat \"/sys/bus/pci/devices/${BDF}/numa_node\"\n```\n\nA non-negative host value must match `PUBLISHED_NUMA`. MIG devices report the\nlocality of their parent GPU and therefore use the parent's PCI bus ID. If the\n--- /Users/bmozaffari/Binaries/NVIDIA/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/site/content/docs/reference/feature-gates.md\n---\ntitle: Feature gates\nlinkTitle: Feature gates\nweight: 20\ndescription: Available feature gates, their stages, defaults, and constraints.\n---\n\nFeature gates control experimental and beta functionality in the DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs. They follow [Kubernetes feature gate conventions](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/command-line-tools-reference/feature-gates/).\n\n## Set feature gates\n\nSet feature gates in your Helm values file:\n\n```yaml\nfeatureGates:\n  TimeSlicingSettings: true\n  MPSSupport: true\n```\n\nOr pass them at install time:\n\n```bash\nhelm install dra-driver-nvidia-gpu oci://registry.k8s.io/dra-driver-nvidia/charts/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu \\\n    --version {{< param \"driver_version\" >}} \\\n    --namespace dra-driver-nvidia-gpu \\\n    --set \"featureGates.TimeSlicingSettings=true\"\n```\n\n## Available feature gates\n\nThe Kubernetes column identifies additional Kubernetes version or Kubernetes feature gate requirements beyond the baseline versions in [Prerequisites](../prerequisites.md).\n\n| Feature gate | Stage | Default | Kubernetes | Description |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `TimeSlicingSettings` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables customization of CUDA time-slicing settings in `GpuConfig`. |\n| `MPSSupport` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables Multi-Process Service (MPS) sharing strategy in `GpuConfig` and `MigDeviceConfig`. |\n| `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | IMEX daemons use DNS names instead of raw IP addresses for peer communication. Required by `ComputeDomainCliques`. |\n| `PassthroughSupport` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables VFIO passthrough device allocation using `VfioDeviceConfig`. |\n| `DynamicMIG` | Alpha | `false` | v1.34 through v1.35 requires `DRAPartitionableDevices=true` on the kube-apiserver and kube-scheduler.  v1.36 and later enables `DRAPartitionableDevices` by default. See [Kubernetes feature gates](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/command-line-tools-reference/feature-gates/#DRAPartitionableDevices). | Enables dynamic MIG device allocation and reconfiguration. See [MIG](../guides/gpu-allocation/mig.md). |\n| `NVMLDeviceHealthCheck` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables GPU health checking using NVML. |\n| `ComputeDomainCliques` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | Uses `ComputeDomainClique` CRD objects to track IMEX daemon membership. Requires `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`. |\n| `CrashOnNVLinkFabricErrors` | Beta | `true` | No requirements. | Causes the kubelet plugin to crash rather than fall back to non-fabric mode when NVLink fabric errors are detected. |\n| `DeviceMetadata` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables IOMMU API device exposure, such as `/dev/iommu` or `/dev/vfio/vfio`, for VFIO workloads with `VfioDeviceConfig`. Requires `PassthroughSupport`. |\n| `FabricManagerPartitioning` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Enables Fabric Manager partition discovery and lifecycle management for full GPUs and VFIO devices on supported HGX and single-node NVL systems. VFIO devices require `PassthroughSupport`. Requires Fabric Manager with `FABRIC_MODE=1`. Each full-GPU or VFIO claim on a participating node must exactly match one published partition. See [Fabric Manager partitioning](../guides/gpu-allocation/fabric-manager-partitioning.md). |\n| `HostManagedIMEXDaemon` | Alpha | `false` | No requirements. | Allows `resources.computeDomains.imex.mode=hostManaged`, where you manage the host `nvidia-imex` service instead of the DRA Driver creating per-ComputeDomain daemon DaemonSets. This gate only unlocks the mode. Driver-managed DNS daemon naming and `ComputeDomainClique` tracking are not used in this mode. See [Validate host-managed IMEX](../guides/compute-domain-workloads.md#validate-host-managed-imex). |\n| `DRAListTypeAttributes` | Alpha | `false` | v1.36 or later with `DRAListTypeAttributes=true` on the kube-apiserver and kube-scheduler. | Publishes list-valued DRA device attributes, including `resource.kubernetes.io/numaNode` as a one-element list. See [NUMA locality](resourceslice-attributes.md#numa-locality). |\n| `ConsumableShares` | Alpha | `false` | v1.34 through v1.35 requires `DRAConsumableCapacity=true` on the kube-apiserver, kube-controller-manager, kube-scheduler, and kubelet; v1.36 and later enables `DRAConsumableCapacity` by default; see [Kubernetes consumable capacity](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/#consumable-capacity). | Implements Kubernetes DRA consumable capacity for full GPUs and MIG devices by publishing them as multi-allocatable and defining their capacity request policies; also set the [`consumableShares`](helm-values.md#feature-gates) Helm value to select an accounting mode; see [Consumable capacity](../guides/gpu-allocation/consumable-capacity.md). |\n\n## Constraints\n\nThe following feature gate combinations are mutually exclusive and cannot be enabled together.\n\n| Combination | Reason |\n| --- | --- |\n| `DynamicMIG` + `PassthroughSupport` | Mutually exclusive |\n| `DynamicMIG` + `MPSSupport` | Mutually exclusive |\n| `PassthroughSupport` + `NVMLDeviceHealthCheck` | Mutually exclusive |\n\nThe feature gates below have the following dependencies:\n\n| Feature gate | Requires |\n| --- | --- |\n| `ComputeDomainCliques` | `IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` |\n| `DeviceMetadata` | `PassthroughSupport` |\n--- /Users/bmozaffari/Binaries/NVIDIA/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/pkg/featuregates/featuregates.go\n/*\nCopyright The Kubernetes Authors\n\nLicensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\");\nyou may not use this file except in compliance with the License.\nYou may obtain a copy of the License at\n\n    https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n\nUnless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software\ndistributed under the License is distributed on an \"AS IS\" BASIS,\nWITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.\nSee the License for the specific language governing permissions and\nlimitations under the License.\n*/\n\npackage featuregates\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\n\tutilruntime \"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/util/runtime\"\n\t\"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/util/version\"\n\t\"k8s.io/component-base/featuregate\"\n\tlogsapi \"k8s.io/component-base/logs/api/v1\"\n)\n\n// featureGateEmulationVersion is the version passed to component-base's versioned\n// feature gate. ResourceSlice). It must be a (node-local) unambiguous device identifier. It's\n// exposed to users in error messages. It's played back to us upon a\n// NodePrepareResources request, which is when we look it up in the\n// `AllocatableDevices` map. Conceptually, this the same as\n// kubeletplugin.Device.DeviceName (documented with 'DeviceName identifies the\n// device inside that pool').\ntype DeviceName = string\n\n// Represents the PCI address (BDF format) of a physical GPU device.\ntype PCIBusID = string\n\ntype AllocatableDevices map[DeviceName]*AllocatableDevice\ntype PerGPUAllocatableDevices struct {\n\tallocatablesMap map[PCIBusID]AllocatableDevices\n}\n\n// AllocatableDevice represents an individual device that can be allocated.\ntype AllocatableDevice struct {\n\tGpu        *GpuInfo\n\tMigDynamic *MigSpec\n\tMigStatic  *MigDeviceInfo\n\tVfio       *VfioDeviceInfo\n\n\t// taints holds DRA device taints set by the health monitor. Published\n\t// as part of the device's ResourceSlice entry so the scheduler and\n\t// kubelet honour them per KEP-5055.\n\ttaints []resourceapi.DeviceTaint\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevice) Type() string {\n\tif d.Gpu != nil {\n\t\treturn GpuDeviceType\n\t}\n\tif d.MigDynamic != nil {\n\t\treturn MigDynamicDeviceType\n\t}\n\tif d.MigStatic != nil {\n\t\treturn MigStaticDeviceType\n\t}\n\tif d.Vfio != nil {\n\t\treturn VfioDeviceType\n\t}\n\treturn UnknownDeviceType\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevice) IsStaticOrDynMigDevice() bool {\n\tswitch d.Type() {\n\tcase MigStaticDeviceType, MigDynamicDeviceType:\n\t\treturn true\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (d *AllocatableDevice) CanonicalName() string {\n\tswitch d.Type() {\n\tcase GpuDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.Gpu.CanonicalName()\n\tcase MigStaticDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.MigStatic.CanonicalName()\n\tcase MigDynamicDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.MigDynamic.CanonicalName()\n\tcase VfioDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.Vfio.CanonicalName()\n\t}\n\tpanic(\"unexpected type for AllocatableDevice\")\n}\n\nfunc (d *AllocatableDevice) GetDevice(config *Config) resourceapi.Device {\n\tvar dev resourceapi.Device\n\tswitch d.Type() {\n\tcase GpuDeviceType:\n\t\tdev = d.Gpu.GetDevice()\n\t\tapplyConsumableShares(&dev, config)\n\tcase MigStaticDeviceType:\n\t\tdev = d.MigStatic.GetDevice()\n\t\tapplyConsumableShares(&dev, config)\n\tcase MigDynamicDeviceType:\n\t\tpanic(\"GetDevice() must currently not be called for MigDynamicDeviceType\")\n\tcase VfioDeviceType:\n\t\t// VFIO passthrough devices do not support consumable shares.\n\t\tdev = d.Vfio.GetDevice()\n\tdefault:\n\t\tpanic(\"unexpected type for AllocatableDevice\")\n\t}\n\tdev.Taints = d.Taints()\n\treturn dev\n}\n\n// UUID() is here for `AllocatableDevices` to implement the `UUIDProvider`\n// interface. Conceptually, at least since introduction of DynamicMIG, some\n// allocatable devices are abstract devices that do not have a UUID before\n// actualization -- hence, the idea of `AllocatableDevices` implementing\n// UUIDProvider is brittle.\nfunc (d AllocatableDevice) UUID() string {\n\tif d.Gpu != nil {\n\t\treturn d.Gpu.UUID\n\t}\n\tif d.MigStatic != nil {\n\t\treturn d.MigStatic.UUID\n\t}\n\tif d.MigDynamic != nil {\n\t\t// For now, the caller must sure to never call UUID() on such a device.\n\t\t// This method for now exists because when the DynamicMIG feature gate\n\t\t// is disabled, `AllocatableDevices` _can_ implement UUIDProvider; and\n\t\t// that feature is used throughout the code base. This needs\n\t\t// restructuring and cleanup.\n\t\tpanic(\"unexpected UUID() call for AllocatableDevice of type MigDynamic\")\n\t}\n\tif d.Vfio != nil {\n\t\treturn d.Vfio.UUID\n\t}\n\tpanic(\"unexpected type for AllocatableDevice\")\n}\n\nfunc (d *AllocatableDevice) GetGPUPCIBusID() string {\n\tswitch d.Type() {\n\tcase GpuDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.Gpu.pciBusID\n\tcase MigStaticDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.MigStatic.parent.pciBusID\n\tcase MigDynamicDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.MigDynamic.Parent.pciBusID\n\tcase VfioDeviceType:\n\t\treturn d.Vfio.PciBusID\n\t}\n\tpanic(\"unexpected type for AllocatableDevice\")\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) GetGPUs() []*AllocatableDevice {\n\tvar devices []*AllocatableDevice\n\tfor _, device := range d {\n\t\tif device.Type() == GpuDeviceType {\n\t\t\tdevices = append(devices, device)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn devices\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) GetMigStaticDevices() []*AllocatableDevice {\n\tvar devices []*AllocatableDevice\n\tfor _, device := range d {\n\t\tif device.Type() == MigStaticDeviceType {\n\t\t\tdevices = append(devices, device)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn devices\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) GetMigDynamicDevices() []*AllocatableDevice {\n\tvar devices []*AllocatableDevice\n\tfor _, device := range d {\n\t\tif device.Type() == MigDynamicDeviceType {\n\t\t\tdevices = append(devices, device)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn devices\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) GetVfioDevices() []*AllocatableDevice {\n\tvar devices []*AllocatableDevice\n\tfor _, device := range d {\n\t\tif device.Type() == VfioDeviceType {\n\t\t\tdevices = append(devices, device)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn devices\n}\n\n// Required for implementing UUIDProvider. Meant to return (only) full GPU UUIDs.\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) GpuUUIDs() []string {\n\tvar uuids []string\n\tfor _, dev := range d {\n\t\tif dev.Type() == GpuDeviceType {\n\t\t\tuuids = append(uuids, dev.UUID())\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tslices.Sort(uuids)\n\treturn uuids\n}\n\n// Required for implementing UUIDProvider. Meant to return MIG device UUIDs.\n// Must not be called when the DynamicMIG featuregate is enabled.\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) MigDeviceUUIDs() []string {\n\tif featuregates.Enabled(featuregates.DynamicMIG) {\n\t\tpanic(\"MigDeviceUUIDs() unexpectedly called (DynamicMIG is enabled)\")\n\t}\n\tvar uuids []string\n\tfor _, dev := range d {\n\t\tif dev.Type() == MigStaticDeviceType {\n\t\t\tuuids = append(uuids, dev.UUID())\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tslices.Sort(uuids)\n\treturn uuids\n}\n\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) VfioDeviceUUIDs() []string {\n\tvar uuids []string\n\tfor _, device := range d {\n\t\tif device.Type() == VfioDeviceType {\n\t\t\tuuids = append(uuids, device.Vfio.UUID)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tslices.Sort(uuids)\n\treturn uuids\n}\n\n// Required for implementing UUIDProvider. Unsure what it's supposed to return for VFIO devices.\nfunc (d AllocatableDevices) UUIDs() []string {\n\tvar uuids []string\n\tfor _, dev := range d {\n\t\tuuids = append(uuids, dev.UUID())\n\t}\n\tslices.Sort(uuids)\n\treturn uuids\n}\n\nfunc (d *PerGPUAllocatableDevices) GetGPUDeviceByPCIBusID(pciBusID string) *AllocatableDevice {\n\tif devices, ok := devs)\n\treturn clone, nil\n}\n\n// Note(JP): for a regular GPU, this canonical name is for example `gpu-0`, with\n// the numerical suffix as of the time of writing reflecting the device minor.\n// NVMLs' DeviceSetMigMode() is documented with 'This API may unbind or reset\n// the device to activate the requested mode.
